- **[ENV-3M7K]** Wave 0 / D1 v1.1: planner auto-partition envelopes across per-section session clips
  `effort: L · impact: S · area: envelope · source: builder · added: 2026-05-19 · status: open · related: MIX-3S7P, AUD-1M4V, ENV-9P4T · stage: ready · refs: .prawduct/artifacts/plans/AUD-1M4V/discovery.md · reviewed: 2026-06-11`

  v1 ships refuse-with-teaching (W10-F) for long envelopes whose range exceeds any single session clip. v1.1: planner detects the multi-clip case, splits the DB's logical envelope at session-clip boundaries, emits one sub-envelope per covering session clip; pull stitches adjacent identical envelopes back. Natural Hallucinote shape. Requires push-side split + pull-side stitch + round-trip test coverage. (Wave 0 triage Group D 2026-05-19)

  **Surfaced as the candidate general answer for MIX-3S7P's deferred song-spanning DubDelay send (2026-06-03).** That gesture must persist across sections where the source track is tacet — the one case the clip-local strategy can't host. Auto-partition (or a monolithic never-tacet host clip) is the general mechanism if/when the user un-defers the dry→wet DubDelay arc. Pending a user creative lock; not pulled into MIX-3S7P.

  **Note (2026-06-10, AUD-1M4V discovery):** scope unchanged — auto-partition remains the answer for song-spanning envelopes over per-section clips; the umbrella's staged plan references it (refs: added per cumulative-Critic finding — it was the only child missing the link).

  **Note (2026-06-11, ENV-9P4T chunk 02):** for the specific *song-spanning-send-across-tacet-gaps* case (MIX-3S7P's deferred DubDelay arc), **perform now offers an alternative route** to auto-partition — an uncovered send envelope routes to a continuous arrangement lane instead of partitioning into per-clip sub-envelopes (commit `cb54ce2`, plain/audio-track perform routing). This item's *session-clip* auto-partition scope is unchanged (ENV-9P4T is explicitly out-of-scope for session-clip auto-partition per its build plan), but the perform path reduces the urgency for the one gesture that drove this item's MIX-3S7P coupling. Still open for the general per-section-session-clip auto-partition mechanism. (ENV-9P4T chunk 02 close-out)

  `effort: L · impact: S · area: envelope · source: builder · added: 2026-05-18 · status: open · stage: requirements · related: AUD-1M4V, ENV-7G4K · refs: .prawduct/artifacts/plans/AUD-1M4V/discovery.md · reviewed: 2026-06-11`

- **[ARR-2S9D]** Energy-realization spectral correlate (the deferred third intensity feature) — DR-3 follow-on
  `effort: S · impact: S · area: energy · source: discovered-from-friction · added: 2026-06-03 · status: open · related: ARR-7M3D · stage: ready · reviewed: 2026-06-09`

  **Flagged deferral from ARR-7M3D (DR-3, NOT a silent drop).** The energy-realization lens ships loudness (LUFS-S median) + onset density as its two intensity correlates. The music-perception literature (ARR-7M3D `research.md` §2/§4 [1][2]) backs a THIRD: spectral density / flux / centroid — a chorus often "opens up" the spectrum at matched loudness, which LUFS alone can't see. Per DISCOVERED-FROM-FRICTION, ship the minimum that closes the headline gap (loudness + density) and add the spectral correlate when friction shows the two miss a real case. The `correlate_rho` dict is open-keyed precisely so a third correlate adds a key (`"spectral"`) without a schema change. **Verifiable signal:** `MixReport.energy_realization.correlate_rho` carries a spectral correlate key computed from a librosa spectral-flux/centroid pass over the windowed master, ranked by the same `realize_energy` Spearman path; OR a decision-record states loudness+density suffice with rationale. **Sized:** small (one new DSP primitive + a measured-correlate map entry; the lens + report shape already accept it). Build when a real song surfaces a matched-loudness spectral lift the lens misses.

  `effort: M · impact: M · area: audio · source: verification · added: 2026-06-02 · status: open · stage: ready · reviewed: 2026-06-09`

So the in-mix RT60 verdicts (A-Plate 3.37 vs intent 3.0; B-Room 1.26 vs 0.8) were measured against a device that ISN'T the authored one — the set was never re-pushed from the rebuilt DB, or the Hybrid Reverb load fell back to stock. Before treating any reverb-vs-intent gap as a real authorship issue: re-push sun-zone-done from the DB so the AUTHORED devices are measured, then re-run render+analysis and re-assess. Also confirm whether the Hybrid Reverb load path is reliable (did the push silently fall back to stock?). **Verifiable signal:** the A-Plate return device class matches `captured_session.json` (Hybrid Reverb) after a fresh push. **Sized:** medium. - **[SNG-D1FF]** "Diffusion" — a denoising-as-form song (+ a target-chord noise-schedule generator)
  `effort: L · impact: M · area: song · source: user · added: 2026-06-03 · status: open · stage: idea · reviewed: 2026-06-09`

  **Concept:** the song is structured like a diffusion/denoising model. There is a single massive
  TARGET chord the whole piece resolves to at the very end. Over the song, the music plays only tiny
  fragments of it — and at the start those fragments are deliberately "wrong" notes (noisy, out-of-chord,
  scattered). As it progresses the fragments coalesce toward the true chord: wrong notes get rarer,
  in-chord notes denser/more confident, until the final reveal lands the full sonority cleanly.

  **Why it's interesting:** form = a denoising schedule. The "noise level" is a real, authorable curve
  (probability of a wrong note + scatter of timing/register), monotonically decreasing — maps naturally
  onto authoring-as-code (a per-section noise parameter that biases pitch selection toward/away from the
  target chord's tones).

  **Open design questions:** what's the target chord (rich/polytonal?); is the schedule linear or does it
  have plateaus/setbacks (a few "reverse-diffusion" moments where it gets noisier for drama?); do rhythm +
  density also denoise (arrhythmic → locked)? single timbre or does instrumentation resolve too? how long
  (the reveal needs runway to feel earned).

  **Possible new capability:** a "target-chord-with-noise-schedule" generator primitive (bias note pitches
  toward a chord by a 0→1 coalescence parameter) — reusable beyond this song. (user idea 2026-06-03)

  **Moved Archive → Open (2026-06-09 triage):** `pending` is not an archive status; this is an open song idea (stage: idea — needs the design questions answered before it is buildable).

- **[DEV-3W9R]** Rack macros are unmodeled — DB representation + push/pull for macro mappings
  `effort: M · impact: H · area: device · source: review · added: 2026-06-09 · status: open · stage: requirements · related: DEV-7K4H, DEV-9K7N · refs: incoming-bugs/2026-06-14-device-param-automation-can-silence-a-part-with-no-warning.md · reviewed: 2026-06-15`

  **BECOMES A FEATURE CHUNK IN DEV-9K7N (NODE-ADDR) (2026-06-15), RE-SCOPED BY PROBE.** Macros are a NODE-ADDR tri-state feature on the rack `device` terminal. **Probe-corrected (2026-06-15, design §1.5):** Live's LOM exposes **no macro→param mapping target** (`has_macro_mappings`/`macros_mapped` are presence-only), so this item's *core ask — authoring a macro→param mapping — is `UNSUPPORTED_IN_LIVE` and cannot be built.** What ships under NODE-ADDR's single release: macro **values + names + variations** (`NOT_IMPLEMENTED` → buildable), depending on the addressing + capture-durability spine (Chunks A/B). Keep this item as the macro-feature requirement record (incl. the silent-part witness below); the mapping-target half is a documented `UNSUPPORTED_IN_LIVE` stub.

  **From the 2026-06-09 repo-wide review.** One macro modulating cutoff + send is a basic sound-design idiom with zero DB representation today. Under "sound design is composition" (`feedback_sound_is_composition`), macro mappings are authorship and belong in the snapshot. Distinct from DEV-7K4H (nested chains). Needs a LOM capability probe for macro-mapping read/write before schema design. **Verifiable signal:** a macro mapping (one macro → ≥2 parameter targets) round-trips DB→push→pull; OR a probe-backed decision-record states the LOM can't express it. (repo-wide review 2026-06-09)

  **Witness (2026-06-14).** The bug report `incoming-bugs/2026-06-14-device-param-automation-can-silence-a-part-with-no-warning.md` is a real-world witness for this item. A `device_parameter` envelope drove a rack macro (Filter Cutoff) into an inaudible Hz band — the lowpass closed onto the fundamental, ~320 Hz — and the part shipped **silent** through a clean build + push (exit 0) + ~6-minute real-time perform pass, caught only by ear. To author a safe cutoff the composer had to `gunzip` the factory `.adg` out of the Live app bundle and read `Filter_Frequency` min/max + the macro default by hand, because the snapshot stores the rack as a bare `preset_query` with no macro ranges/curve. This **escalates impact from M → H**: not merely "can't author a basic macro idiom" but "silently ships an inaudible part." **The fix to author against is exactly this item:** capture macro ranges + the macro→param mapping into the snapshot so authors can convert Hz↔macro without spelunking the `.adg`.

  Triage (2026-06-14) **DESCOPED** the bug report's two lighter directions:
  - **(a) A generalized `assert_param_audible` build helper** — redundant with the per-song build-time assertion the song already added (`test_voice_filter_cutoff_never_closes` + the build-time `AssertionError` in `songs/swell/build.py`), and meaningless without this item's macro→Hz curve (you can't assert an audible floor without knowing what a macro value maps to in Hz). It collapses into this item.
  - **(b) Perform/render silence detection as a gate** — rejected on ruler-vs-stamp grounds (`feedback_great_art_not_software`): a near-silent part is valid art (ambient beds, breakdowns, fade-outs, sidechain ducking), so a silence *gate* would veto legitimate music. Defensible only as a non-verdict mix-review *surface*, and even there redundant with the existing per-part loudness read in the MixReport. Not a separate backlog item.

The item is now thin create handlers + the push/pull surface for CLP-AUD1's audio-clip rows. See `.prawduct/artifacts/plans/AUD-1M4V/discovery.md`.

Current interim behavior CLP-AUD2 replaces with real handlers: push **refuses `kind='audio'` loudly** (teaching error, no silent skip) and pull **exempts audio rows from reconcile** — both are deliberate placeholders for this item's create handlers + push/pull surface.

The only path is async browser-load: set `song.view.highlighted_clip_slot = target_slot`, then `application.browser.load_item(audio_browser_item)`. Caveats: async (no completion callback), audio must be addressable as a BrowserItem (Library/User/Places — not arbitrary filesystem path), browser-indexing dependent. Could expose as `ableton_clip(action='load_audio_to_session', track_index, clip_index, browser_uri)`.

- **[TRK-2H6K]** Group tracks (`tracks.parent_track_id` + Live group semantics)
  `effort: L · impact: S · area: track · source: user · added: 2026-05-17 · status: open · stage: idea · related: RTE-1K9T · reviewed: 2026-06-12`

  (migrated from legacy P6)

  **DEFERRED (user, 2026-06-12) — gated on a hard LOM blocker.** Live's LOM has **NO `create_group_track`**: groups are a UI-only operation (Cmd+G) and cannot be created programmatically. The LOM only *reads* group membership (`track.group_track` exposes the parent), but it cannot *create* a group track. This is the gating fact for the item. Deferred in favor of **RTE-1K9T**'s plain-audio-bus routing (a plain audio PRE-MAIN bus + track output routing), which delivers the master-like bus this item was wanted for **without needing a group** — so the group-track capability is no longer on the critical path. Kept open at `stage: idea`; revisit only if the LOM gains a group-create surface or a group (vs a plain bus) becomes specifically required.

- **[DEV-5R8Q]** No replace-instrument-keep-FX-chain path — device load is append-only, Live 12.4 has no reorder API
  `effort: M · impact: M · area: device · source: dogfood · added: 2026-06-10 · status: open · stage: requirements · related: SNP-8R4K · refs: incoming-bugs/archives/2026-06-10-swell-first-compose-friction.md · reviewed: 2026-06-11`

  From the swell friction log (item 3). Swapping a track's instrument while keeping its FX chain has no supported path: device load is append-only and Live 12.4 exposes no device-reorder API, so a replaced instrument lands at the END of the chain. Candidate fix: a `rebuild_chain` convenience (delete-descending + reload-in-order from the `devices[]` spec) — or, at minimum, document the rebuild pattern in the conventions guide. **Verifiable signal:** either `rebuild_chain` exists and a replace-instrument round-trip leaves the FX chain order intact, or the conventions guide documents the delete-descending + reload-in-order pattern.

  **Update (2026-06-11, CLR-A chunk-05 decision) — documentation half SHIPPED, `rebuild_chain` convenience DEFERRED with rationale.** The verifiable-signal *disjunct* is now met on the documentation side: the **delete-descending / reload-in-order chain-rebuild pattern is DOCUMENTED** in the conventions guide ("Reordering / inserting mid-chain"), shipping with the CLR-A PR. The `rebuild_chain` **convenience is consciously DEFERRED** — it is NOT a pure-planner emission: the push planner binds devices idempotently by class+position with no "reorder existing chain" diff, so a convenience would need new Remote-Script-side orchestration to sequence delete+reload and manage the transient-empty-chain window. Kept `open` as a future convenience (the manual pattern is the sanctioned interim); revisit when a song forces the round-trip enough to earn the Remote-Script orchestration.

- **[MCP-9R3T]** `ableton_render` must be ASYNC (start+poll) — a 60s MCP-call cap can NEVER fit a realtime multi-minute render, so every real render returns a FALSE FAILURE today
  `effort: M · impact: H · area: mcp · source: dogfood · added: 2026-06-18 · status: open · stage: ready · related: MCP-5N8K, DEV-6T2W · refs: .prawduct/artifacts/plans/MCP-ASYNC-RENDER-ANALYZE/build-plan.md, incoming-bugs/archives/2026-06-13-async-render-capability-and-skill-design.md, incoming-bugs/archives/2026-06-14-render-analyze-60s-false-failure-dominant-tax.md · reviewed: 2026-06-18`

  **HIGH-priority transport bug (swell mix dogfood; carried from MCP-4T6Y, which this + MCP-5N8K supersede).** `ableton_render` is **inherently realtime / multi-minute** (≈98s playback for 190 beats + ring-out), so the **per-MCP-call 60s ceiling can essentially NEVER fit a real render**. Today renders **do finish + write a well-formed `manifest.json` (status: ok)** — but the caller gets a **FALSE FAILURE** (error returned client-side while the work actually succeeded) with **no completion signal**, forcing a filesystem poll for `manifest.json` to discover the render is done. This makes the async fix not nice-to-have but **necessary for `render` to be usable at all** without polling workarounds. **HIGH because it always fails today.**

  **The fix = make `render` ASYNC via START + POLL** (design verified at planning; supersedes the original MCP-4T6Y "notify-when-done" sketch). **DESIGN PIVOT (verified):** the original plan to *notify-when-done via the host background-task channel* is **INFEASIBLE in Claude Code** — there is no async/wake-on-done for MCP tools; MCP progress notifications **do not reset the tool-call timeout** (Claude Code bug #58687), and that timeout is a **client-side, transport-agnostic wall-clock limit** — identical for stdio / HTTP+SSE / Streamable HTTP, so HTTP/SSE transport was considered and **rejected** (it does not move the ceiling). The feasible design is **START + POLL**: a `start` action returns immediately with a **job handle** (`{render_id, captures_dir, expected_stop_beat, eta_seconds}` — tiny payload, never holds the socket for the realtime duration) **plus INSTRUCTION TEXT** telling the agent how to poll a `status` action; `status(render_id)` **long-polls ~45s** and returns `{state: running|done|failed, progress_beats, captures_dir, error?}` (tiny, no file lists). Raising the per-server `.mcp.json` timeout is a **complementary lever, not the fix**. `start` must not block other MCP calls — if Live's single-threaded LOM forbids true concurrency during a transport pass, at minimum reject/queue concurrent calls with an instant `{busy: true, render_id}` rather than hanging them to a 60s timeout.

  **Keystone risk (decided by Chunk 1's verify-api spike on real Live):** whether the render job can be **detached** in Live's single-threaded Remote Script — **mechanism A** (server-side detached render) vs **mechanism B** (agent-driven long-poll fallback). Chunk 1 of the build plan **is this item's thin-vertical-slice de-risk** (keystone item). Both mechanisms remain **server-side** (MCP `_FINGERPRINT_PATHS`) + **Live-gated**.

  **What's actually missing (narrowing the build):** the **render socket is already unbounded** and `_wait_for_capture` **already writes a `status.json` heartbeat** — so the false-failure is purely the **outer Claude Code tool timeout**, not the render itself. What's missing is **`start` (background) + `status` (action) + the instruction text** that teaches the agent to poll.

  **Server-side + Live-gated.** The change lives in the `hallucinote_mcp` render handler and is in the MCP `_FINGERPRINT_PATHS` (any wire-shape change flips the server fingerprint — relaunch dev-mode then `/ableton-mcp-install` to push dev→Live). Verification is **Live-gated** (a real realtime render against an open set).

  **Interim workaround that works today:** call `render` (ignore the false failure), background a bash loop waiting on `manifest.json`, then proceed (e.g. run `analyze` in-process — see MCP-5N8K).

  **Follow-on (shared with MCP-5N8K): a `/render-analyze` skill** orchestrates render→wait-on-completion→analyze entirely OUT of the agent's main context and returns ONLY the MixReport summary + report_path. Depends on BOTH async render (this) and async analyze (MCP-5N8K) — see the skill note on MCP-5N8K.

  **Verifiable signal:** `render` runs as a START + POLL async action (`start`→`{render_id, eta}` + instruction text; `status` long-polls ~45s and returns running|done|failed), no false-failure, the socket is not held for the realtime duration, and a concurrent `session(info)` during an in-flight render does not hang to timeout.

- **[MCP-5N8K]** `ableton_analysis(analyze)` must be ASYNC (start+poll) — a many-surface song exceeds 60s and writes NO report (genuine failure); analysis time is UNBOUNDED in track count
  `effort: M · impact: H · area: mcp · source: dogfood · added: 2026-06-18 · status: open · stage: ready · related: MCP-9R3T, DEV-6T2W · refs: .prawduct/artifacts/plans/MCP-ASYNC-RENDER-ANALYZE/build-plan.md, incoming-bugs/archives/2026-06-13-analyze-action-exceeds-60s-mcp-timeout-multi-stem.md, incoming-bugs/archives/2026-06-14-render-analyze-60s-false-failure-dominant-tax.md · reviewed: 2026-06-18`

  **HIGH-priority transport bug (swell mix dogfood; carried from MCP-4T6Y, which this + MCP-9R3T supersede).** `ableton_analysis(action='analyze')` on a many-surface song (e.g. **23 surfaces**: loudness+attribution+masking+timing+cross-rhythm, all auto-enabled when sections are declared) **exceeds the per-MCP-call 60s ceiling and NO report is written** — a genuine failure (unlike render's false-failure). Yet the *same* handler called in-process from Python with a longer budget completes cleanly and writes the report, **so the DSP is fine; the bottleneck is purely the transport ceiling**, and analysis time is **UNBOUNDED in track count** (per-stem passes + cross-surface masking/attribution terms, O(stems)→O(stems²)). Most *finished* songs are 15–25 tracks, so this hits **essentially every real (sectioned) song at mix-review time** — precisely when the tool is most needed; not a swell edge case. **Also:** a long action **blocks unrelated MCP calls** (`session(info)` timed out at 60s simply because the server was busy driving the analysis) — so `start` must not hang concurrent calls. **HIGH because it always fails on real (sectioned) songs.**

  **The fix = make `analyze` ASYNC via START + POLL** (same verified design as MCP-9R3T; supersedes the original MCP-4T6Y "notify-when-done" sketch). **DESIGN PIVOT (verified):** the original plan to *notify-when-done via the host background-task channel* is **INFEASIBLE in Claude Code** — no async/wake-on-done for MCP tools; MCP progress notifications **do not reset the tool-call timeout** (Claude Code bug #58687); the timeout is a **client-side, transport-agnostic wall-clock limit** (same for stdio / HTTP+SSE / Streamable HTTP, so HTTP/SSE was considered & rejected). The feasible design is **START + POLL**: a `start` action returns immediately with a **job handle** (`{job_id, captures_dir, eta_seconds}` — tiny, never holds the socket) **plus INSTRUCTION TEXT** telling the agent how to poll; `status(job_id)` **long-polls ~45s** returning `{state: running|done|failed, progress, report_path?, error?}` (tiny, no full report inline). Raising the per-server `.mcp.json` timeout is a **complementary lever, not the fix**. `start` **must not block other MCP calls** — if Live's single-threaded execution forbids true concurrency, at minimum reject/queue concurrent calls with an instant `{busy: true, job_id}` rather than hanging them to a 60s timeout (what `session(info)` did mid-analysis).

  **Chunk 2 of the build plan (lower risk than MCP-9R3T's keystone):** analysis is **pure DSP in the MCP server process — no Live threading** (unlike render's detached-job keystone risk), so it **reuses the Chunk-1 job substrate** (the `start`/`status` machinery proven in Chunk 1). The **disposition decision is settled in Chunk 2**: whether to keep a **synchronous `analyze` as a fast path for small jobs** vs **always-async**. Server-side (MCP `_FINGERPRINT_PATHS`) + Live-gated. The shared keystone-mechanism risk (detached render — mechanism A — vs agent-driven long-poll — mechanism B) is owned by Chunk 1; analyze does not re-incur it.

  **Server-side + Live-gated.** The change lives in the `hallucinote_mcp` analysis handler and is in the MCP `_FINGERPRINT_PATHS` (any wire-shape change flips the server fingerprint — relaunch dev-mode then `/ableton-mcp-install` to push dev→Live). Verification is **Live-gated** (a real many-surface analysis against rendered captures).

  **Interim workaround that works today:** run `analyze` **in-process** from Python with a longer budget (the DSP completes and writes the report); pair with MCP-9R3T's render workaround (call `render`, ignore the false failure, background a bash loop on `manifest.json`, then analyze in-process).

  **Follow-on (shared with MCP-9R3T): a `/render-analyze` skill** (the least-context-polluting path): `/render-analyze [start_beat] [stop_beat] [--compare <seq>]` orchestrates render→wait-on-completion→analyze entirely OUT of the agent's main context and returns to the agent **only** the MixReport `summary` (true-peak, overshoot count, per-section count, out-of-tolerance reverbs) + `report_path` + an optional one-line delta vs `--compare`. Full per-stem/per-band JSON stays on disk for selective reads. Net agent context for a full verify cycle: one skill call in, one ~6-line summary out (vs today's timed-out render error + filesystem poll + manual in-process analyze + hand-written extraction scripts). **Depends on BOTH async actions** (MCP-9R3T render + this analyze) — build the two async actions first, then the skill on top.

  **Verifiable signal:** `analyze` runs as a START + POLL async action (`start`→`{job_id, eta}` + instruction text; `status` long-polls ~45s and returns running|done|failed), a concurrent `session(info)` during an in-flight analysis does not hang to timeout, the report is written reliably regardless of surface count, and (follow-on) a `/render-analyze` skill returns ONLY the MixReport summary + report_path (no WAV lists / full JSON in agent context).

- **[STR-9P4M]** Stereo positioning + movement as an intent-graded review dimension (compose-review + mix-review)
  `effort: M · impact: M · area: analysis · source: user · added: 2026-06-11 · status: open · related: MIX-3S7P, MEL-1A7K, ARR-8P5K · stage: idea`

  **Raised by the user (2026-06-11, dogfood).** `/compose-review` and `/mix-review` should consider **stereo positioning and movement** — pan placement (static field per part) AND panning movement (panning automation / autopan / stereo-width movement over time) — measured per part/section and judged against song/section/part intent. Today the review lenses read masking, bed buildup, loudness, attribution, reverb, timing/feel, cross-rhythm, melody, recurrence — but **nothing reads the stereo field**: where each part sits in the image, how wide it is, and how (whether) it moves.

  **The house discipline applies verbatim (this is a 4th/5th sibling of the declared-intent-lens pattern — masking / melody / harmony-lint / performance).** Like masking and melody, there is **no universal "correct" stereo pattern** — the lens MEASURES the realized stereo field per part/section (pan position, stereo width, and any pan/width movement over time) and GRADES it against the song's **DECLARED** intent (e.g. *"drums wide, vocal dead-center, the pad should drift left-to-right across the break"*), surfaces the gap as a **producer's question, never a verdict or a score**, and **learns revealed intent back per-song** (the markdown-annotation mechanism `/mix-review` + `/compose-review` already use). A part panned hard-left when the intent was "centered" reads *"you placed the lead hard-left here — was that the move, or should it sit center?"*, never *"wrong."* The composer/LLM owns the spatial idea (ruler-not-stamp); the lens never auto-pans.

  **Both sides + the two surfaces.** Authoring is a **declared stereo INTENT** per part/section (placement / width / movement-shape), parallel to `MelodicProfile`; the read side is the analysis lens. It plausibly lands on BOTH review surfaces with different inputs: **compose-review** can read intended placement/movement symbolically from the arrangement + pan/width envelopes in `build.py` (render-free, like the melody + recurrence lenses); **mix-review** reads the *realized* stereo field from the render/MixReport (the audio ground-truth — couples to AUD-8H2M, the analyzer's time-varying-mix-move blind spot, for verifying that authored pan/width movement actually landed). Couples tightly to **MIX-3S7P** (which AUTHORS per-section pan/send automation — this item READS+GRADES it) and to the dimension taxonomy (**ARR-8P5K** — is "stereo field" a structure-intent axis, a realization-layer read, or a subsystem? settle the placement there).

  **Stage `idea` (needs discovery before code, per Principle 6):** open questions — the declared-intent SCHEMA (placement scalar? width? a movement-shape vocabulary mirroring the energy curve / contour shapes?), the grain (per-part / per-section / per-bar), whether compose-review's symbolic read and mix-review's audio read are one lens with two adapters or two lenses, and the MixReport stereo metrics needed on the audio side. A research/requirements pass (likely a deep-research survey of stereo-field perception + width/correlation metrics, mirroring the melody + performance passes) precedes the both-sides build. **Verifiable signal:** a stereo-field analysis lens exists (read side, graded against DECLARED per-song stereo intent, info-question never verdict) wired into compose-review and/or mix-review, AND a declared-stereo-intent authoring surface exists OR a decision-record states stereo correctness stays composer-owned with rationale; surfaces a "you intended X, the field reads Y — intended?" question and learns the revealed intent back as a per-song annotation. **Sized:** medium (one lens + intent schema + adapter + finding, on the proven declared-intent-lens pattern; discovery-gated). (user stereo-review dogfood request, 2026-06-11)

- **[ENV-9P4T]** Performed automation at mix scale — plain-track mixer targets + single-pass batched recording
  `effort: L · impact: L · area: envelope · source: user · added: 2026-06-11 · status: open · related: ENV-7G4K, AUD-1M4V, MIX-3S7P, ENV-3M7K, ENV-8H1T · stage: ready · refs: .prawduct/artifacts/plans/ENV-9P4T/build-plan.md, .prawduct/artifacts/plans/ENV-9P4T/discovery.md, .prawduct/artifacts/plans/ENV-7G4K/design.md, .prawduct/artifacts/plans/AUD-1M4V/discovery.md · reviewed: 2026-06-11`

  Extends **ENV-7G4K** (performed automation, shipped — master/group/return mixer/sends/device-params) and **AUD-1M4V R3.2**. **Problem:** a song with 10+ tracks each needing continuous volume+pan (+device/send) rides can't get them today — a plain track's own mixer automation only routes through the per-clip **session-clip path** (segments at clip boundaries, MIDI-only — the path MIX-3S7P / MIX-7K2D author against), while the **perform path** that writes continuous arrangement lanes is scoped to master/group/return AND plays the transport once **PER arc** (N arcs = N full playthroughs). So neither path gives a plain track a clip-boundary-independent continuous arrangement ride, and recording many arcs costs N×song-length wall-clock.

  **Success:** (1) `perform` accepts **plain-track** `mixer_volume`/`mixer_pan` so any track gets a continuous arrangement-view ride independent of clip boundaries; (2) **one push records ALL changed performed arcs across all tracks in a single transport run-through** (wall-clock ≈ one song length, not N×); (3) a documented **safety stance** for arrangement automation the user hand-edited in Live — perform is **write-only** (no LOM read), so re-record can silently clobber manual edits.

  **Out of scope:** tempo/signature automation (LOM-blocked, separate); building live-LOM **read** of arrangement automation (no surface — the `.als` dump is the known read path, named not built); audio-clip placement (CLP-AUD2); session-clip auto-partition (ENV-3M7K).

  **Update (2026-06-11): stage design→ready — build plan authored** at `.prawduct/artifacts/plans/ENV-9P4T/build-plan.md` (discovery at `.prawduct/artifacts/plans/ENV-9P4T/discovery.md`). **3 chunks:** (1) **single-pass batched recording** — record ALL changed performed arcs across all tracks in one transport run-through (the perf keystone; kills the N×song-length wall-clock cost); (2) **plain-track + audio-track perform targets** — the 10+-track continuous-ride capability; folds **ENV-8H1T**'s perform-side AND unlocks the deferred **MIX-3S7P** / **ENV-3M7K** song-spanning send; (3) **fidelity via adaptive sampling density**. **Scope:** write-side only — harvest / `.als`-read explicitly DEFERRED per user (perform stays write-only; the documented safety stance is the contract). (user mix-scale performed-automation request, 2026-06-11)

  **Update (2026-06-11b): chunk 02 SHIPPED — plain/audio-track perform routing** (commit `cb54ce2`, `feat(automation): ENV-9P4T chunk 02 — plain/audio-track perform routing`, infer-from-span). `perform` now routes plain- and audio-track mixer targets to a continuous arrangement-view lane (the 10+-track continuous-ride capability), inferring the lane span from the envelope's breakpoint range rather than clip boundaries. This DISCHARGES the chunk-02 dependents named in the build plan: ENV-8H1T's perform side is addressed (only the session-clip-side residual remains, gated on CLP-AUD2), and the deferred MIX-3S7P / ENV-3M7K song-spanning send is now plan-routable (an uncovered send envelope routes to a continuous arrangement lane). Chunks 01 (batched recording) shipped earlier; chunk 03 (adaptive sampling density) remains. Item stays `open` for chunk 03. (ENV-9P4T chunk 02 close-out)

  **Update (2026-06-11c): MERGED to develop (PR #161) — RELEASE-PENDING; the "stays open for chunk 03" reason above is now STALE.** Chunks 01 (single-pass batched recording) + 02 (plain/audio-track perform routing) are MERGED to develop via **PR #161** (squash `57a6fe0`; change-log entry `status=merged`). Chunk 03 (fidelity) was **consciously DESCOPED**, not deferred-open: the chunk-03 verify-api probe proved the framed adaptive-tick-density approach unrealizable — perform-path fidelity is scheduling-bound (~2.5 Hz realtime floor, no direct-write surface on the perform target's `DeviceParameter`), so denser authoring can't yield denser recording. The achievable lever (tempo-reduction-during-record) was spun out as **ENV-2T9K**; the perform-handler hardening carryovers as **ENV-8K2R**. The feature's success criteria are MET: plain-/audio-track continuous-ride targets, single-pass batched recording (≈ one song length, not N×), and the documented write-only safety stance. So this is NOT open-for-more-work — it is **release-pending** (merged to develop, `status=merged` in the change-log, awaiting the develop→main release). Remaining perform-path follow-ups live in their own items (ENV-2T9K fidelity, ENV-8K2R hardening), not under this id. (ENV-9P4T post-merge reconciliation, PR #161)

- **[ENV-2T9K]** Perform fidelity via tempo-reduction-during-record (the redirected ENV-9P4T chunk 03)
  `effort: M · impact: M · area: envelope · source: builder · added: 2026-06-11 · status: open · related: ENV-9P4T, ENV-7G4K · stage: design`

  **The redirected scope of ENV-9P4T chunk 03.** The chunk's framed approach — "fidelity via adaptive sampling density" (adaptive tick density) — was **invalidated by the chunk-03 verify-api probe**: perform-path automation fidelity is fundamentally **realtime-bound (~2.5 Hz, scheduling-bound)**. A 0.5-beat dip authored to a depth of 0.1 records back to 0.589 (the breakpoints land where the wall-clock tick happens to fire, not where authored), and the `DeviceParameter` exposes **only `begin_gesture`/`end_gesture` — no direct-write surface** (live-confirmed). Adaptive ticking can't beat the scheduling floor: the tick rate is fixed, so denser *authoring* doesn't yield denser *recording*.

  **The sound redirect.** Temporarily **lower the transport tempo during the `perform_batch` pass** so the fixed ~2.5 Hz wall-clock tick rate yields proportionally **more breakpoints PER BEAT**. Because `FloatEvent`s are **beat-keyed**, the captured automation plays back correctly at the song's real tempo — the slowdown is a recording-time trick, invisible in the result. 4× slower tempo ⇒ 4× breakpoint density per beat.

  **Why it's M, not trivial.** A non-trivial `perform_batch_handler` change: **global-tempo save/restore mid-pass** (set low before recording the arcs, restore after — must survive the exception path), **cost-model interaction** (the wall-clock estimate must account for the slowdown), and **optionally adaptive per arc steepness** (steeper arcs get a deeper slowdown). It carries a real **wall-clock cost**: 4× slower tempo = 4× density = **4× wall-clock** for that pass — a deliberate fidelity/time trade. Its Live-smoke needs an **`/mcp` reconnect (server respawn)** to pick up the handler change, so it's **operator-gated** (can't be fully unattended).

  **Separate, smaller possibility (note, not the main scope):** the **undocumented `create_event` / `AutomationEnvelope` API** may reach **SESSION-CLIP** envelopes (NOT perform/arrangement lanes — those are the gesture-record path this item improves). If it does, it's a possible fidelity win **for the session-clip route only** (direct event writes, no scheduling floor). Worth a quick probe **only if session-clip envelope fidelity ever matters** — orthogonal to the perform-path tempo-reduction work above.

  **Verifiable signal:** `perform_batch_handler` lowers + restores the transport tempo around the arc-recording pass (save/restore survives the exception path); a 0.5-beat dip authored to 0.1 records back materially closer to 0.1 than the ~0.589 baseline at the reduced tempo; the cost model reflects the slowdown's wall-clock multiplier. **Sized:** medium (handler change + tempo save/restore + cost-model wiring + optional per-arc adaptivity; operator-gated Live smoke). (ENV-9P4T chunk-03 verify-api probe redirect, 2026-06-11)

- **[ENV-8K2R]** ENV-9P4T perform-handler hardening (cumulative-Critic carryovers)
  `effort: M · impact: M · area: envelope · source: critic · added: 2026-06-11 · status: open · related: ENV-9P4T, ENV-2T9K, ENV-7G4K, PSH-3H8M · stage: ready · reviewed: 2026-06-13`

  Consolidated perform-handler hardening from the ENV-9P4T cumulative Critic. Most items touch `hallucinote_mcp` `perform_batch_handler` and need an **`/mcp` reconnect (server respawn)** to live-verify the handler's recording behavior, so they were deferred from the ENV-9P4T PR (operator-gated; can't be fully unattended). The **`/mcp`-reconnect rationale applies specifically to the handler-RECORDING items — 1, 3, 5** (the `session_automation_record` disarm, the staggered duplicate-target collision, and the `perform_batch` wire-read ceiling — these change runtime recording behavior that only a respawned server + a live transport pass can exercise). Item **6 is the EXCEPTION** (see its note below): it is purely test-side plus one `server.py` import line, fully covered by unit tests, so it needs **no `/mcp` reconnect** and can be picked up **independently / sooner** than the handler-recording work. Two data-safety/fidelity fixes lead; the rest are code-health + robustness.

  **Checklist (priority-ordered):**

  1. **(data-safety, highest)** `session_automation_record` async restore needs a settle-verify. **EMPIRICALLY CONFIRMED async 2026-06-12** (set `True` → immediate read `False` → later read `True`), so the bare `setattr` disarm at the restore path has a **silent unapplied window** — the next playback could silently record clip envelopes. **Fix:** parametrize `_wait_for_record_mode_on_worker` over the attribute name and settle-verify `session_automation_record` too, calling the helper **DIRECTLY on the worker thread** (NOT through `_attempt`'s `run_on_main` — that nests `run_on_main` from the main thread and **deadlocks**).

  2. **(fidelity)** Gesture close never pins the arc's final breakpoint value (`_write_or_close`: `beat >= span_end` closes without a value write) — the recorded endpoint is up to **~0.8 beat short** of the authored final. **Fix:** write `interp(span_end)` immediately before `end_gesture`. Relates to ENV-2T9K.

  3. **(correctness)** Staggered duplicate-wire-target collision **bypasses the planner duplicate-target preflight silently** (`perform.py:299-343`) — `queued_targets` is seeded only with fingerprint-surviving arcs, so a skipped-unchanged arc colliding with a changed arc on one wire target records with **no alert and corrupts the stored fingerprint**. **Fix:** seed the preflight with skipped-unchanged `perform_target_key`s.

  4. **(robustness)** The apply layer doesn't cross-check planned vs returned arcs (`plan.py` `perform_batch` branch) — a short/empty arcs list records nothing silently. **Fix:** add a count/ID cross-check warning.

  5. **(robustness)** Unbounded `perform_batch` wire read has no client-side ceiling — if the worker dies without responding while TCP stays open, `push_cli` blocks **forever**. **Fix:** a planner-derived explicit `read_timeout` (union-span estimate × 3 + settle) restores a ceiling while preserving verification.

  6. **(code-health, no-back-compat-to-throwaway)** `server.py` re-imports private `_DEFAULT_READ_TIMEOUT` / `_ENSURE_LOADED_READ_TIMEOUT` solely so `test_server.py` legacy assertions pass — back-compat to throwaway in-repo tests. **Fix:** repoint `test_server.py` at the client (public `read_timeout_for`), keep one test that `handle_tool_call` CONSULTS the policy, drop the private re-exports. **No `/mcp` reconnect needed (unlike items 1, 3, 5):** unlike the handler-recording fixes, this one is purely **test-side plus a single `server.py` import line** — it changes no runtime recording behavior, so the unit tests cover it in full and it can be **picked up independently / sooner** without an operator-gated server respawn.

  7. **(code-health, dedup)** `_resolve_perform_target` inlines send/return resolution a **4th time** (`handlers/automation.py:~1586-1622`). **Fix:** extract `_resolve_send` / delegate to `_require_parent` **before ENV-2T9K's handler change spreads it.**

  **Verifiable signal:** the `session_automation_record` disarm settle-verifies (no silent recording window), gesture close writes the final breakpoint value, the duplicate-target preflight catches staggered skipped-vs-changed collisions, the apply layer warns on planned/returned arc mismatch, `perform_batch` carries a client-side read ceiling, `server.py` no longer re-exports the private timeout constants, and send/return resolution is extracted to one helper. **Sized:** medium. (ENV-9P4T cumulative-Critic carryover, 2026-06-11)

<!-- Automation-ingest spike findings (READ side) — filed 2026-06-12 from .prawduct/artifacts/research-spike-automation-ingest.md. The spike's READ/ingest complement to the write-side perform items (ENV-9P4T/ENV-7G4K, which explicitly DEFERRED harvest/read). The unifying architecture is L0-capture (4 interchangeable sources) → L1 mechanical reconciler (NO LLM, classify+bucket vs DB) → L2 delta summary to the LLM. ING-1R4C is L1 (the spine); ING-2S7K / ING-3V9D / ING-4P2M / ING-5W8H are the four L0 sources. -->

- **[ING-1R4C]** L1 mechanical reconciler — diff captured automation from the DB, classify + bucket, summarize (the spine; NO LLM)
  `effort: L · impact: L · area: automation-ingest · source: user · added: 2026-06-12 · status: open · related: ING-2S7K, ING-3V9D, ING-4P2M, ING-5W8H, AUD-1M4V, SNG-7H4M, ENV-9P4T · stage: design · refs: .prawduct/artifacts/research-spike-automation-ingest.md`

  **The architecture's spine (operator + agent synthesis, 2026-06-12 — see the SYNTHESIS ⚡ block in the artifact).** Capture mechanisms are interchangeable **L0 data SOURCES** (ING-2S7K/3V9D/4P2M/5W8H); the raw events MUST NOT reach the LLM (thousands of events). The reconciler is a purely **mechanical, render-free, NO-LLM** layer between them: **normalize** each source to a common form (per target → breakpoints in beats; sampled sources pass through step-detection first) → **compare** to the DB's authored envelopes → **classify** each target `{MATCH · DRIFT · MISSING · UNEXPECTED · OVERRIDDEN (automation_state=2)}` → **bucket** by **track × arrangement-section** (via the DB's section map) → emit a compact structured report. **L2** (the LLM-facing summary) scales with the *delta*, not the data volume — a healthy push → near-zero tokens; surfaces only divergence in MUSICAL coordinates ("Track 3 cutoff, chorus 2 — drifted"), never raw beats. This is the **MixReport pattern for CONTROL data** — the moat is that Hallucinote KNOWS what it pushed (the DB), so capture becomes *reconciliation* and reconciliation collapses thousands of events into a handful of deltas. Generic DAW capture has nothing to diff against.

  **Two modes, same L0 sources:** **VERIFY** (capture vs DB → delta summary; the sanity-check / map) and **INGEST** (capture → normalized envelopes → DB via mutators, events fall out → LLM sees "ingested N envelopes across M tracks," not breakpoints; an imported song has no DB reference yet → ingest is reduce-and-write, not diff). **Seed already on disk:** `src/hallucinote/sync/pull/envelopes.py` (709 LOC) already does `value_at_time` sampling + step-transition detection + a curve-preservation MERGE (match captured bp ↔ DB bp by time within tolerance, inherit `curve_kind`) — **that merge IS the L1 kernel**; generalize it into classify-and-bucket.

  **Hard parts (→ requirements before code, why `stage: design` not `ready`):** (1) **TOLERANCE must model KNOWN fidelity** — diff against **expected-rendered** (DB curve through the known capture/perform-fidelity model), NOT idealized-authored, else performed automation's measured ~2.5 Hz staircase ALWAYS false-flags as DRIFT. (2) **COVERAGE** — sampled/passive sources only see played/scrubbed spans; the reconciler must record which time-ranges were covered and report MISSING only WITHIN them, else unplayed sections false-alarm as "didn't fire." (3) **NORMALIZATION** — sampled sources (scrub, passive) need step-detection to become breakpoints; `events_in_range`/XML are already breakpoints; converge to one form before L1. **Verifiable signal:** a render-free reconciler module exists that takes (normalized captured envelopes, DB envelopes, section map) and returns a classify-and-bucket delta report with the five classes, against the expected-rendered (not idealized) reference, coverage-gated for MISSING; a test exercises a healthy-push (near-zero delta) and each divergence class. **Sized:** large (the architecture's core; reuses the pull/envelopes merge kernel). (automation-ingest spike synthesis, user, 2026-06-12)

- **[ING-2S7K]** L0 source — scrub-read ingest of arrangement/master automation (transport-stopped, sampled, arbitrary resolution)
  `effort: M · impact: L · area: automation-ingest · source: user · added: 2026-06-12 · status: open · related: ING-1R4C, ING-3V9D, AUD-1M4V, SNG-7H4M · stage: design · refs: .prawduct/artifacts/research-spike-automation-ingest.md`

  **PROVEN in-session, NO RS shim required (decisive probe, 2026-06-12 — see the SCRUB-READ ⚡ blocks).** The mechanism that reaches the targets the event API can't: arrangement and master automation have **no `Envelope` object** to enumerate, but **setting `current_song_time` while the transport is STOPPED moves the playhead and `parameter.value` updates to the automation value at that position.** Probe-confirmed on a master-volume ramp (beat 0→0.850 · 2→0.714 · 4→0.460, monotonic) and characterized at sub-beat resolution (1.0→0.806 vs 1.25→0.714 are distinct → ≤0.25-beat discriminates; finer available, limited only by sample count). So a non-realtime "dump X→Y" reaches arrangement/master via **scrub-and-read** at **arbitrary resolution** (NOT bound by perform's ~2.5 Hz), through the EXISTING `set current_song_time` + `get …value` probe verbs — no `Envelope`, no realtime, no RS shim.

  **Key constraints to design around:** (1) **Async settle** — `set current_song_time` applies ~asynchronously; the immediate read-back is STALE. A scrub loop MUST let the seek settle (a tick) before reading (confirmed: `set=4`→stale-0, then `set=2` reporting `old=4.0`). (2) **Sampled, not lossless** — scrub-read returns the value-EXACT *rendered* value at your scrub grid (no scrub-introduced error), but it's a SAMPLED reconstruction (like `value_at_time`), NOT the original authored breakpoints/curve-type; for true-lossless breakpoint times + curve kinds, ING-5W8H (`.als` XML) wins. For an ingested **user-drawn** curve, fine scrubbing faithfully recovers the drawn shape. (3) **Throughput, not capability** — over MCP, N samples = N `set`+`get` round-trips with settle (~10 calls for 6 points); whole-song × many params is impractical per-sample → the production form is an **in-RS scrub loop** that reads the whole buffer on the main thread and returns in ONE call. This is a perf upgrade, NOT a capability gap — the capability works through the probe today. **Resolution policy:** an adaptive grid (coarse, then refine where the value changes — exactly the existing `value_at_time` step-detector in `sync/pull/envelopes.py`) minimizes samples. Feeds the L1 reconciler (ING-1R4C) after step-detection normalization. **Verifiable signal:** an ingest path that scrubs `current_song_time` across a span (settle-gated) and reads `parameter.value` for arrangement/master targets, recovering a known authored ramp within the grid resolution; the in-RS whole-buffer loop is the production form. **Sized:** medium (the v1-sufficient in-session ingest of automation *values*; in-RS loop is the throughput follow-on). (automation-ingest spike, scrub-read confirmed, user, 2026-06-12)

- **[ING-3V9D]** L0 source — `events_in_range` lossless session-clip read + `create_event` curved write (the session-clip fidelity upgrade)
  `effort: M · impact: M · area: automation-ingest · source: user · added: 2026-06-12 · status: open · related: ING-1R4C, ING-2S7K, ENV-9P4T · stage: design · refs: .prawduct/artifacts/research-spike-automation-ingest.md`

  **The undocumented envelope-event API — reach map COMPLETE, reclassified as a session-clip fidelity upgrade (probed 2026-06-12).** `AutomationEnvelope.events_in_range(t0,t1) -> EnvelopeEventVector` / `create_event(EnvelopeEvent)` / `delete_events_in_range` EXIST and `events_in_range` **READS losslessly** (returned real `EnvelopeEvent`s on a one-step envelope — lossless breakpoint enumeration is real, correcting the 2026-06-11 "no breakpoint-enumeration API" claim). **BUT the `Envelope` object is obtainable for SESSION CLIPS + same-track params ONLY** — master, return, cross-track, AND arrangement clips all raise `RuntimeError: Not a session clip or parameter belongs to another track`. **Therefore this is NOT an ingest mechanism** for "a song that already has automation" (that means *arrangement* automation, which is refused) — it is a **fidelity upgrade for the session-clip automation Hallucinote already authors**: (a) lossless READ (`events_in_range` replaces `value_at_time` sampling) and (b) CURVED WRITE (`create_event` with `ControlCoefficients` replaces `insert_step`, fixing the "curves recorded as hints but applied as `hold`" stepped-only limitation, `sync/push/envelopes.py`).

  **Blocked-on-harness (the OPEN probe → why `stage: design`):** the curved-WRITE round-trip is unproven — `create_event` needs an `EnvelopeEvent` *instance* arg (`EnvelopeEvent(ControlCoefficients|Vector)`, RS-only, no public signature) that the `ableton_probe` `$path` grammar can't construct, and returned `EnvelopeEvent`s have no LOM path to `describe` for field introspection. **Unblock = run code that imports the class inside the RS** (a throwaway shim or this item's real handler change) + a full **Live quit+reopen** (Control-Surface module cache). Recommendation from the spike: **this + ING-4P2M (passive telemetry) are ONE in-RS build** (a new branch, plan + Critic) — they share the in-RS-code + Live-reload cost, and a single reload can validate `create_event` round-trip AND listener resolution together. **Note vs ENV-9P4T:** PSH-/ENV- already noted the `create_event` session-clip possibility at backlog.md:586 as orthogonal to the perform path — this item is that probe, scoped. **Verifiable signal:** a reach-map decision-record + either (i) `events_in_range` adopted for lossless session-clip envelope read, and/or (ii) `create_event` curved write proven round-trip (curves survive the `.als` dump) and adopted to replace `insert_step` for session-clip automation; explicitly NOT extended to arrangement/master (refused). **Sized:** medium (read is adoptable now; curved write needs the in-RS shim + Live reload). (automation-ingest spike, event-API reach map, user, 2026-06-12)

- **[ING-4P2M]** L0 source — passive automation telemetry rider (in-RS listeners; VERIFY/map, not ingest)
  `effort: L · impact: M · area: automation-ingest · source: user · added: 2026-06-12 · status: open · related: ING-1R4C, ING-3V9D, AUD-8H2M, ENV-9P4T · stage: requirements · refs: .prawduct/artifacts/research-spike-automation-ingest.md`

  **A passive capture that rides the playback already happening (operator, 2026-06-12 — see the PASSIVE TELEMETRY ⚡ block).** The mix/render workflow plays the song repeatedly; passively capture what the engine renders during those passes — buffered in-RS, drained in ONE response at pass-end — for **(i) sanity-check** that automation fired as authored and **(ii) map** where automation is active. Distinct PURPOSE from scrub-read/`events_in_range` (which OWN ingest): this is **observation/VERIFY**, not ingest — they are complementary. **Mechanism, two tiers:** `add_automation_state_listener` = cheap **MAP** (which params' automation engages + when — 0→1→2 transitions, no values); `add_value_listener` = rich **SANITY-CHECK** (atomic `(song_time, value)` captured on Live's main thread at every engine-emitted change — the highest-fidelity in-session capture, essentially free during a pass, no MCP-timing slop because timestamps are captured at listener-fire, not by polling).

  **Killer integration:** rides the **SAME pass** as `ableton_render` / `ableton_analysis` (`research-spike-audio-analysis.md`) — it is the **control-rate sibling** of the M4L audio telemetry (analyzer streams audio features; the RS captures automation value/state; the two are cross-referenceable: "the cutoff move at bar 33 ↔ the spectral shift the analyzer saw"). Feeds the L1 reconciler's VERIFY mode (ING-1R4C) — strongest on the EXPECTED(DB)-vs-ACTUAL(rendered) delta (catches push failures, manual overrides `automation_state=2`, drift, and DB-absent automation). **Boundary vs scrub-read (so we don't build overlap):** passive = cheap, broad, continuous **SENTINEL** that flags "something changed / didn't as expected"; scrub-read / `events_in_range` = precise on-demand **PROBE** of a specific span. Natural pipeline: passive flags → scrub-read confirms exactly.

  **Cost / honesty (why `stage: requirements`):** this is the ONE source that genuinely needs the **in-RS shim + Live reload** (listeners take a Python *callable*; the probe can't register one) — now JUSTIFIED by ongoing value, and shares the in-RS build with ING-3V9D's `create_event`. Coverage = only what you play (biased toward replayed/early sections — the reconciler must coverage-gate). Captures *rendered* (post-everything) values — right for "what the audience hears" verification, NOT for lossless breakpoint preservation. **Design Qs → requirements before code:** watch-list scope (all params vs a declared list — reuse the analyzer's track/param enumeration); listener lifecycle + leak-safe teardown; drain trigger (transport-stop detection vs explicit MCP drain); buffer cap; the render-pass integration point; the main-thread-callback → worker-harvest plumbing (the `threading.Event` + worker-blocks-on-main pattern `dispatch.py:105` already uses, widened to a playback window). **Verifiable signal:** a design note for the capture-session/listener primitive (lifecycle + drain + watch-list) + an in-RS passive-capture that drains `(song_time, value)` and automation-state transitions in one pass-end response, feeding ING-1R4C's VERIFY mode. **Sized:** large (real stateful in-RS surface + the reconciler integration). (automation-ingest spike, passive telemetry, user, 2026-06-12)

- **[ING-5W8H]** L0 source — `.als` XML cold ingest (offline, lossless, reaches everything incl. master/arrangement)
  `effort: L · impact: M · area: automation-ingest · source: user · added: 2026-06-12 · status: open · related: ING-1R4C, ING-2S7K, SNG-7H4M, AUD-1M4V · stage: research · refs: .prawduct/artifacts/research-spike-automation-ingest.md`

  **The only TRUE-lossless ingest source — and the only one that reaches everything offline (spike Lane 1.1 / §1 row C; viability CONFIRMED — lom-rec doc + hodel33/ableton-project-processor in the wild).** Parse the gzipped `.als` project file with **Live CLOSED**: it carries the real breakpoints + curve KINDS for clip envelopes, arrangement automation, AND master/return mixer automation — everything the in-session sources sample or can't reach. Reach map (artifact §1): `events_in_range` = session-clip only; scrub-read = arrangement/master but sampled; passive = only what's played; **`.als` XML = lossless for ALL targets** (`MasterTrack > AutomationEnvelopes`, clip envelopes, arrangement lanes). It's the ground-truth oracle the spike used to characterize the other sources' fidelity.

  **Honest constraints (why `stage: research`):** (1) **Live must be CLOSED** — out-of-band, can't run mid-session; fits **cold ingest** of an existing project file, NOT live sync. (2) **Version-fragile** — the XML schema drifts across Live releases; a parser is ongoing maintenance. (3) **Schema specifics are OPEN** (the artifact's named research gap): the exact XML paths for clip envelopes (not just mixer), the curve/coefficient encoding, arrangement vs clip-local time normalization, and round-trip integrity — mine hodel33/ableton-project-processor and build a throwaway parser over one real `.als` with KNOWN automation, diffing extracted breakpoints against what we authored (Lane 1.1, all offline, no Live). Normalizes (already breakpoints — no step-detection needed) into the L1 reconciler (ING-1R4C); the strongest cold-ingest candidate for SNG-7H4M (`/song-import`) and the AUD-1M4V vocal/audio-ingest umbrella. **Verifiable signal:** a research note recording the confirmed `.als` XML paths + curve encoding for clip/arrangement/master envelopes (mined from hodel33 + validated on one real set), feeding a parser that extracts lossless breakpoints+curve-kinds and normalizes them for the L1 reconciler; round-trip integrity measured against authored ground truth. **Sized:** large (schema research → parser → normalization; version-maintenance is ongoing). (automation-ingest spike, .als XML cold ingest, user, 2026-06-12)

- **[ING-9H2T]** Research hand-ridden Live automation read-back — the recorded-performance leg's binding constraint
  `effort: ? · impact: M · area: automation-ingest · source: user · added: 2026-06-16 · status: open · stage: research · related: ING-1R4C, ING-2S7K, ING-4P2M, ING-5W8H · refs: .prawduct/artifacts/authorship-model.md`

  When a human records volume/pan (or any) automation by **riding faders** in Live's arrangement, it lands as **arrangement automation**, which has **no LOM read surface and no enumeration** (`ableton://guides/gaps` — "write-only … no read surface"; `ableton_automation action='list'` unsupported). So it **cannot be synced back into the song today** — this is the **recorded-performance authorship leg's binding constraint** (authorship-model.md open problem #1: "Hand-ridden automation read-back (LOM wall)").

  **Research candidate routes:** (1) coax Ableton to **play the ride out** so it's captured as MIDI/events; (2) other creative **LOM routes**; (3) **worst-case parse the `.als` file** (last resort — avoid). These map onto the existing automation-ingest L0 sources — route 1 is the play-out sibling of **ING-4P2M** (passive telemetry rider) / scrub-read **ING-2S7K**, route 3 is **ING-5W8H** (`.als` XML cold ingest); this item is the **authorship-model-side framing** of that read-back problem (the *why it matters* leg), to be reconciled with the ING cluster's *mechanism* items before any build. Stage: research/discovery — direction unresolved, needs live tests (see authorship-model.md §"Open problems" #1). (user, authorship-model open problem #1, 2026-06-16)

<!-- Push-execute operability cluster — filed 2026-06-13 from the swell-push dogfood (3 of the 5 incoming-bugs reports). All three concern the OPERABILITY of `push_cli execute` for long/realtime pushes (the ~8–11 min `performed_automation` perform dominates): phase-targeting for cheap recovery (PSH-2R7K), mid-run progress so a long run isn't a black box (PSH-5T9D), and a transport watchdog so a non-advancing perform fails clean instead of hanging forever (PSH-3H8M). Mutually reinforcing and a direct operational mitigation for the RTE-2P9X phase-ordering halt. -->

- **[PSH-3H8M]** `performed_automation` (`perform_batch`) hangs indefinitely when the transport won't advance — needs a transport watchdog + pre-perform reset
  `effort: M · impact: M · area: sync · source: dogfood · added: 2026-06-13 · status: open · stage: ready · related: ENV-8K2R, PSH-2R7K, PSH-5T9D · refs: incoming-bugs/archives/2026-06-13-perform-batch-hangs-on-non-advancing-transport.md · reviewed: 2026-06-13`

  **Severity M, swell-push dogfood 2026-06-13.** A hung perform blocks the rest of the push (`arrangement`, `cues` never run) AND blocks recovery: you can't tell it's hung vs. legitimately playing the ~8–11 min span, and the only exit is `kill -9` (which leaves more dirty state). **Repro:** (1) a push reached `performed_automation` playing the master-swell span; the user manually **stopped the transport** mid-record → the `execute` process wedged (had to kill); (2) the **next** `execute`'s perform phase **never advanced** — transport frozen at a fixed position, `perform_batch` waiting, process `STAT=S` at **0.0% CPU** for 4.5 min, output buffer empty, no timeout/error/progress. Diagnosis: `perform_batch` issued the play + recorded-span wait, but the transport never moved (residual state from the interrupted prior perform), so the call blocked forever.

  **The robustness gap.** `perform_batch` assumes the transport will traverse the span and return; it has **no watchdog** for "transport position hasn't advanced in N seconds" and **no upper bound** ("span is M beats ≈ T seconds at tempo; abort at, say, 1.5×T"). Any transport-stalling condition — user stop, modal dialog, residual state from an interrupted perform — turns into an indefinite hang instead of a clean teaching failure. **Proposed fix:** (1) **watchdog** — poll `song.current_song_time` during the record wait; if it hasn't advanced for N seconds (and `is_playing` is false/unexpectedly stopped), abort with a structured error ("transport stopped advancing at beat X of the Y-beat span — perform aborted; check for a manual stop / modal dialog / restart Live to clear residual transport state"); (2) **hard ceiling** — derive expected wall-clock from span-beats / tempo and abort at a multiple; (3) **pre-perform reset** — before playing, force a known-clean transport state (stop + reposition to span start; clear loop/punch) so a prior interrupted perform can't poison the next one (would have prevented step 2 entirely).

  **Note — distinct from but adjacent to ENV-8K2R item 5.** ENV-8K2R item 5 adds a *client-side* `push_cli` TCP **read** ceiling (worker dies, socket stays open → `push_cli` blocks forever). This item is the complementary *server-side* gap: the worker is alive and waiting but the **transport itself won't advance**, so no response is ever produced for the client to read. Both are needed — the read ceiling bounds a dead-worker hang, the watchdog/ceiling/reset bounds a stalled-transport hang and produces a *diagnostic* abort. Pairs naturally with PSH-5T9D (even a single "perform: beat X/Y" heartbeat would have made the hang obvious in seconds) and PSH-2R7K (`--start-at performed_automation` would make the clean retry one command). **Verifiable signal:** stopping the transport mid-perform (or starting a perform with a wedged transport) ends in a structured abort within seconds-to-watchdog, not an indefinite 0%-CPU hang; the pre-perform reset clears residual transport state so a clean retry succeeds; `arrangement`/`cues` either run on retry or the failure names the cause. **Sized:** medium (watchdog poll + ceiling derivation + pre-perform transport reset in `perform_batch_handler`; operator-gated Live smoke needing an `/mcp` reconnect). (swell-push dogfood, 2026-06-13)

  **Discovery while building the PSH cluster (2026-06-13 — DEFERRED this session, NOT shipped).** Two of the three proposed fixes substantially already exist in the perform handler: (a) a **hard wall-clock ceiling** — `automation.py` ~1953–1981: `deadline = wall_start + max(expected_s * _PERFORM_WALL_CLOCK_FACTOR, FLOOR) + settle`; the ramp loop raises `TimeoutError` past it (covers proposed-fix 2); and (b) a **partial pre-perform reset** — `_arm_and_seek` ~1922–1933 stops if playing + seeks to `union_start` before arming (covers part of proposed-fix 3). The **genuine residual**: the observed hang was flat **0% CPU for 4.5 min** — that is the worker **BLOCKED inside `run_on_main(_ramp_step)`** (Live's main thread not servicing the callback). Both the existing deadline and a worker-side non-advancement watchdog run only **BETWEEN** `run_on_main` calls, so **neither can fire** while blocked inside one. The real fix is therefore a **timeout on `run_on_main` itself** — a core primitive used everywhere, so **high blast radius**, and only reproducible/tunable against real Live. A worker-side **non-advancement watchdog** (track last beat + last-advance time; abort if `current_song_time` hasn't moved for N seconds) would additionally help the *lesser* responsive-but-frozen sub-case (proposed-fix 1). **Both need a Live session to validate thresholds — do not implement blind.** Note **PSH-5T9D layer-3** (intra-perform beat heartbeat) is the same server-side/Live-gated surface — **pair them**. **Still open because:** server-side (`handlers/automation.py`, in the MCP `_FINGERPRINT_PATHS`) ⇒ changes the server fingerprint + needs a full Live re-verification of the perform path; out of scope for the engine-only PSH-2R7K / PSH-5T9D(1–2) that shipped this session. (PSH cluster build, 2026-06-13)

  **CONFIRMED-DEFERRED — re-verified 2026-06-13 (verify-first triage).** Re-read the live code: two of the three proposed fixes already exist — the hard wall-clock ceiling (`handlers/automation.py` ~1956–1981) and the pre-perform reset (`_arm_and_seek` ~1922–1932). The genuine residual is a **timeout on `run_on_main` itself**: the 0%-CPU hang blocks INSIDE `run_on_main`, so neither the deadline nor a worker-side watchdog (both run between callbacks) can fire. That is a high-blast-radius core primitive in `_FINGERPRINT_PATHS`, only tunable against real Live — NOT an autonomous-no-Live target. Kept open at this residual scope.
